Harness balances enter by faucet-claim advances without the admission that precedes them in production. |

### 6.11 One receipt on the BLE confirm, headers that name only the sender, no forward commitment, no signed balance anchor (branch `fix/beta-skeleton-compile`, 2026-09-24)

Owner rulings #4 (remove `min_state_number`), #6 (a field no receiver verifies does not stay on the wire) and #7 (remove `Balance.state_hash` from the signed canonical representation), and the ruling of 2026-09-24 on local answers (a response this device's Core or SDK gives its own caller makes no sender claim and carries no headers).

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk` · bluetooth/bilateral_ble_handler.rs · `handle_confirm_request`; `proto` · `BilateralConfirmRequest` | The receiver checked the sender's parent and child paths separately against roots the confirm carried beside the receipt (fields 3, 4, 5, 9), never bound those roots to the receipt, and fed the unbound roots to the offline-bearer predicate with a zero fallback. | The receiver decodes the receipt once, refuses one not from this session's sender to this device, and runs `verify_receipt_state` against the Device Tree root it keeps for the sender; the bearer predicate takes its roots from that receipt. Fields 3, 4, 5, 9 reserved. Test: `a_confirm_whose_receipt_does_not_hold_is_refused_before_the_ek_check` (the state-rule call removed → red; the device binding removed → red). |
| `dsm_sdk` · bluetooth/bilateral_ble_handler.rs · confirm and commit | Zero stood in for a missing receiver challenge, root, parent tip and policy id; an unresolved receiver policy commit became empty deltas left for the conservation guard to refuse; unreadable cert-chain heads, anchor frontiers and contact Device Tree roots read as absent (an unreadable frontier meant "first transfer, adopt the release's root"); the sender's commit, lacking a signed receipt, archived one rebuilt without signatures. | Each is a refusal or an error naming what is missing. The sender refuses to commit without the receiver's counter-signed receipt; it checks that receipt's state rules against the Device Tree root it keeps for the receiver. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/receipts.rs · `build_bilateral_receipt_with_smt`; storage/client_db/contacts.rs · `get_contact_device_tree_root` | The builder returned `None` with the reason only logged; the Device Tree root read turned a closed database, a malformed id and a corrupt blob into "no root". The local Device Tree commitment was derived from the device id when the app state held none. | `Result` on both; the local commitment comes from the app state or is an error. |
| `proto` · `Headers.chain_tip` (2), `Headers.seq` (4) | The tip was zero from the SDK, the relationship's tip on the b0x path, and a hash of an error string from the Core bridge; the one reader, the previous-tip route filter, compared it with the held tip. `seq` counted nothing a receiver read. | Reserved; the parser refuses both tags and requires `genesis_hash`. The inbox address hashes the relationship's tip (storage node spec §8), so a previous-tip route item is non-adjacent by its route: never applied, consumed when already accepted. The SDK's header counter is removed. Tests: `strict_decode_refuses_the_reserved_header_tags`, `strict_decode_requires_the_genesis_hash` (each gate removed → red). |
| `proto` · `Invoke.pre_state_hash` (4), `Invoke.post_state_hash` (5); `OnlineTransferRequest.chain_tip` (8) | Written as zeros, or read into a `B0xEntry` field nothing read. | Reserved; `B0xEntry.sender_chain_tip`/`next_chain_tip` and `B0xSubmissionParams.next_chain_tip` removed; `wallet.sendSmart` no longer resolves a tip only that field carried. |
| `dsm` · commitments/precommit.rs, types/state_types.rs (ruling #4) | `ForwardLinkedCommitment` (its signer id hard-coded to `"entity"`), `EmbeddedCommitment`, `PreCommitment.forward_commitment`, `State.forward_commitment` / `StateParams::with_forward_commitment` and the state-types `PreCommitment` carrying `min_state_number` — nothing in production set any of them, yet `State::to_bytes` encoded the absent commitment. `encode_forward_commitment_params`, `generate_forward_commitment_verification` and the `ForwardCommitment` error variants had no producer. | Deleted with their hash domain; the recovery `carry_forward_commitment` is a different concept and stays. |
| `dsm` · types/token_types.rs, types/operations.rs (ruling #7) | Every operation amount signed a 32-byte state hash: `wallet.send`, the inbox decoder and the BLE hint path stamped `H(DSM/balance-anchor, ε)`, a constant naming no state; other paths a zero hash; `Balance::zero()` stamped a fallback hash from a thread-local context nothing set. | An operation signs an amount as value and lock (`Balance::canonical_amount_bytes`, 16 bytes) and decodes exactly that; operations are built with `Balance::amount`, which references no state. A held balance still records the state it was derived from (`balance_from_projection`). The anchor, the thread-local context and both hash domains removed. Tests: `an_amounts_state_reference_is_not_signed` (encoding restored → red), `an_amount_carrying_a_state_reference_does_not_decode` (length check relaxed → red). |
| `frontend` · public/vectors/v1, src/vectors/kv.ts | The v1 corpus #962 deleted everywhere else: no reader, request bytes carrying the removed header tags, cases 0007/0008 driven by `headers.seq` fault injection. | Deleted with its parser. |
| local answers · `dsm` core/bridge.rs; `dsm_sdk` ingress.rs, jni/helpers.rs, jni/unified_protobuf_bridge.rs, platform/ios/transport.rs | Responses to this device's own caller carried invented senders: a device id hashed from the message id or the error text, zero identities, derived message ids; the Core bridge and JNI domain tags existed only for that. `isErrorEnvelope` decoded answers as addressed envelopes, so an app-router answer (already headerless) never read as an error. | One local-answer form (`dsm::envelope::local_answer`, `local_answer_from_canonical_bytes`): no headers, no message id, never sealed, one payload; an addressed envelope and a local answer do not decode as each other. The six tags removed. Test: `a_local_answer_and_an_addressed_envelope_do_not_cross` (the local-answer header refusal removed → red). |

Owner rulings #2 (no debug-build bypasses; dev nodes run the production path with a generated local CA, certificates and tokens) and #3 (an explicit schema version, exact-match startup, fresh databases only, no in-place migration; schema version, protocol version and register incarnation are separate axes).

A database without a version marker was treated as adoptable; the local test database held three tables no current code creates. The unwritten `objects` table fed a legacy Device Tree fallback read. | `SCHEMA_VERSION` 1: an empty database is created at it in one transaction with its version row; a database at it must hold exactly `SCHEMA_LAYOUT` and `SCHEMA_INDEXES`; tables without a version, another version, or a drifted layout are refused. The backfill, the `objects` table, its fallback read and its hash domain removed. Tests: `an_empty_database_is_created_at_the_schema_version`, `a_database_with_tables_and_no_version_is_refused`, `a_database_at_another_version_is_refused`, `a_database_whose_layout_is_not_the_versions_is_refused` (each check removed → red). |

Tests: `with_no_admin_token_configured_no_one_is_admitted`, `gossip_admits_only_the_configured_token_holder` (opt-out restored → red). |

Tests: `a_config_missing_a_required_setting_is_refused_by_name`, `a_missing_config_file_is_refused` (a default restored → red; file made optional → red). |

### 6.13 ByteCommit ancestry in route-chain evidence, one write per missing value, no mint after genesis (branch `fix/route-chain-evidence`, 2026-09-24)

Auditor findings on `61d273a3`: the ByteCommit's parent link was not part of the evidence (1), the mirror evidence threshold was unstated (2), `from_leader` rewrote values the leader held (3), and post-genesis mint vocabulary and branches remained (5).

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm` · route_chain.rs · `CommittedAt`, `committed_by`, `leader_seen_at` (MR-DSM-0079, MR-STOR-0094) | A ByteCommit backed a link when the record was included under its root; its chain link was never checked, so a ByteCommit with any `parent_digest` counted. | `CommittedAt` carries the member's ByteCommit at the previous cycle (`None` at cycle 1). `chain_link_holds` requires a zero parent digest at cycle 1 and `follows(parent)` after it, and is part of `commits`, the one check both readers use. `dsm_sdk` · sdk/route_seats.rs · `committed_at` fetches the parent from the same mirrors. Test: `route_chain::tests::a_byte_commit_backs_a_link_only_when_it_follows_its_parent` (valid → Final; bent `parent_digest` → `LeaderLinkUncommitted`; orphan at cycle 2 → not Final). Chain-link check removed → red. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/route_seats.rs · `agreed` (storage spec §3, §14 rule 3) | The number of mirrors whose agreement makes a ByteCommit evidence was not stated. | Not a threshold: the storage fault model is crash-only (§3: a node never equivocates or alters what it stores), and a ByteCommit is never accepted by counting mirrors (§14 rule 3). One mirror's copy is taken and the verifier checks its chain link and root. Stated at `agreed`. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/route_seats.rs · `from_leader` (MR-DSM-0083, MR-DSM-0213) | The batch to the leader rewrote every value, including those the leader already held. | Only the values with no link at the leader are written. ### 6.15 An unestablished candidate is never read as absence (branch `fix/unestablished-candidate-is-not-absence`, 2026-09-24)

| `dsm` · sofi/storage.rs · `keep_verifying`, `keep_all_verifying`; `lean4/DSMSofiStorage.lean` · `keep`, `keepAll` | A candidate under a locator whose bytes fewer than three members returned was skipped as if examined, and a scan in which nothing verified answered `Resolved::None`: an unestablished candidate read as "nothing is published here". The Lean model had the same skip, and `within_budget_exhausted_is_none` proved it. | `keep_verifying` is `Unavailable` when it kept nothing past an unestablished candidate (a verifying candidate elsewhere is still kept: the identity is the locator). `keep_all_verifying` returns `Discovered::Complete` only when every candidate was examined and established, else `Partial` with what verified. Lean: `keep` and `keepAll` rewritten to match, `within_budget_exhausted_is_none` now requires every candidate established, and `an_unestablished_candidate_is_never_none` and `an_unestablished_candidate_makes_discovery_partial` proved. The Lean mutation (the old skip restored) breaks `kept_verifies` and the new theorem. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/sofi_evidence.rs · `fetch_vault_genesis`; sdk/sofi_flow.rs · `own_setup_ref` | A vault whose genesis candidate could not be fetched was reported `NotPublished`; a device whose setup candidate could not be fetched was refused as having no admitted setup. | Both answer "not published" only on a `Complete` discovery; a `Partial` one is a storage error. Node-backed tests on Postgres: `an_unestablished_genesis_candidate_is_not_read_as_unpublished`, `a_setup_scan_that_met_an_unestablished_candidate_is_not_a_refusal`. Each gate removed → its test red; each Core flag removed → its test red. |

### 6.17 The signer set authorizes only what the policy's own rules name (branch `fix/signer-set-authorizes-only-what-the-policy-names`, 2026-09-24)

MR-SOFI-0310, SoFi §47 and §49: a policy's signer set and threshold authorize only what the policy's own rules name, and the standard release rule names none; §54: the burn flag governs burns only; Amendment S8: creation is the creator device's.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk` · handlers/token_routes.rs · `derive_policy_file`; `dsm` · types/policy_types.rs, core/token/policy/policy_enforcement.rs, policy_validation.rs; `proto` · `TokenAuthorityProto` (oneof 9) | Every token was given a `TokenAuthority` condition requiring the signer set's k-of-n signatures over a witness for burn and creation: a second authorization channel no policy rule names. The burn flag was expressed only for non-transferable tokens; a transferable, burn-disabled token was protected from burns only by that witness. `Burn.proof_of_ownership` and the witness in `CreateToken.signature` existed for that condition alone. | The condition, its preimage, its witness keys, the `(pk, sig)` witness record, `token_authorization_witness` and `insert_auth_witness` are deleted; oneof field 9 reserved. Every token's conditions are its `SupplyCap` and one `OperationRestriction` built from its two flags (`permitted_operations`: `create_token`; `transfer`, `lock`, `unlock` if transferable; `burn` if burn-enabled). `Burn` loses `proof_of_ownership` (a wire change to the canonical operation bytes); `CreateToken` is signed as every operation is, by the device key over its cleared canonical bytes. The signer set stays in the committed blob (§47) and authorizes nothing until a release rule names it. |

### 6.21 Resolution reads nothing about what the bytes refute, and a trade cut short is the network status (branch `fix/sofi-resolution-in-hand-and-network-status`, 2026-09-24)

§5A "SoFi resolution and conformance": no SDK test drove an in-hand refutation through the resolver, none produced `RetriesExhausted`, and `resolve_recognized` read the registration a second time on the refuted path.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/sofi_resolve.rs` · `resolve_recognized` | The trader's own position, refuted in hand, read its registration again — the one read `resolve_pending_position` had already made to find the exercise. `resolve_trader_position` had no caller. | `resolve_recognized` takes the registration its caller read; `facts_of` reads it only when no caller has. A refuted exercise reads nothing more (MR-DSM-0041, MR-DSM-0042). `resolve_trader_position` deleted. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/handlers/node_e2e_tests.rs` | No test drove an in-hand refutation on the nodes. | `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_key_held_by_an_exercise_its_own_bytes_refute_is_skipped_on_those_bytes_alone`: B's own exercise re-aimed at the vault's next generation and signed again by B — one operation's exercise, signed by the trader it names, and refuted by its own bytes (conformance item 7, `LegsDoNotMatchPreimage`: P's legs are not the legs its P(E) derives) — is written final at the next generation's first key by any party; the walk skips it with the nodes asked for the two attempt keys and their ByteCommit material and nothing else (the harness records every request each node serves); B's next trade takes attempt 1 and realizes at the reserves the first trade left. With the in-hand refutation bypassed the nodes are asked for registration cells and objects: red. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/storage/client_db/sofi_vault_head.rs`, `client_db/mod.rs` (schema 19), `sdk/sofi_evidence.rs` | **Found by the test above: a trader's second trade against a vault could not resolve.** The vault head store kept only the head's leaves, replaced wholesale. Resolving the device's own position first walks the vault chain, which classifies the device's own exercise consumed and records the generation it produced; the resolution then needed the leaves of the generation the exercise was built on, the store answered with the head's, the root did not match, and `RouteValidation` was missing a vault leaf every round: `RetriesExhausted` forever. Only a trade at a vault's genesis generation could resolve. | `sofi_vault_leaf` is keyed by generation: every generation this device established keeps its whole tree, and `leaves_at(vault, root)` serves the generation the operation names. `record_resolved_with_conn` derives generation `g + 1` from generation `g`'s set. Client schema 18 → 19 (beta: reset, no migration). With the store answering the head's generation whatever root is asked: red. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/handlers/node_e2e_tests.rs`, `test_support/nodes.rs` | No test produced `RetriesExhausted`. | `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_trade_cut_short_by_a_refused_write_is_the_network_status_until_it_lands`: the harness can make one member's store refuse named cell writes (a trigger on the node's own `cells` table; the put fails whole, the member answers an error, every read serves). The pair refused at its leader: the trade is `RetriesExhausted` at `q` with the position fenced, nothing admitted, no balance moved (since §6.31; until then an error). The pair landing and the first leg's key refused at its leader: `sofi.resolve` is `RetriesExhausted` at `q`, still pending, nothing moved. Every write landing: `Realized` at `q`, balances moved, the head agrees with the admitted root. Mutation controls: §6.31. |

### 6.22 Gates that could not fail (branch `fix/ci-gates-that-cannot-fail`, 2026-09-25)

The shortcut audit of 2026-09-25 ("Gates that cannot fail", "What CI tests is not what ships") re-checked the CI gates. Each row below was confirmed by running the gate in this tree before the change.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `ci/canonical_bilateral_gates.sh` | Its four checks passed whatever the code did: the allow-globs were passed to `rg` without `--glob`, so `rg` treated `!**/*test*.rs` as missing paths and exited 2 under `2>/dev/null`; the script printed dozens of `PairingState` / `initiate_pairing` hits and then `✓`, exit 0 (run and confirmed). Check 4 reported `✓ Contact existence gating present` for a Kotlin file that does not exist. What the checks banned — the words `pairing`, `EnvelopeV2`, `store_contact…ble` — is not a protocol rule anywhere in `specs/`, and the one real rule (only QR acceptance creates a contact) is held in code, where `store_contact` is unreachable except from the acceptance predicate. | Deleted, with its `ci.yml` step. A word ban is not a gate. |
| `ci/block_bluetooth_mods.sh` | An unconditional `exit 0` that nothing invoked; the BLE README cited a `ci_gates.sh` that does not exist. | Deleted. The README names the gate that exists (`scripts/check_forbidden_symbols.sh`) and states that no gate checks transport timers against protocol semantics: `ci/no_clock_and_no_json.sh` allowlists the BLE transport files. |
| `ci/bridge_contracts_gate.sh` | The strict frontend roots named `WebViewBridge.ts`, which became a folder; `rg` exited 2 on the missing path, `if rg …` read that as "no match", and all five bridge bans passed while the folder was never scanned. | The roots are the folder and `BridgeGate.ts`; every root must exist; `rg`'s exit 2 is a failed scan (exit 2), only exit 1 passes. Run on the real folder: no hits. The gate also runs in the Frontend job now, with `npm run lint`, so a frontend-only change meets it (`ci/layers.toml` never selected `rust-gates` for one). |
| `ci/no_clock_and_no_json.sh` | Exited 0 with a `SKIPPED` line when `rg` was absent. | `rg` absent is a failed gate (exit 1). |
| `scripts/ci_scan.sh` | `fail_if_found` treated `rg`'s exit 2 as a pass, so a rule pinned to a deleted file could never fire: two were (`routing_path_sdk.rs`, `route_commit_sdk.rs`, both gone). | Exit 2 is an error (exit 1); the two rules over deleted files are deleted. |
| `ci/sofi_no_default_evidence.sh`, `ci/admitted_predecessor_readers_fenced.sh`, `ci/sofi_reachability.py` | "Production code" was everything before a file's FIRST `#[cfg(test)]`; a file with an early test item had the rest of its production code never scanned (`formal_infra.rs`: 1,619 of 32,053 bytes). | `ci/production_text.py` removes every `#[cfg(test)]`-attributed item (brace-matched block or `;` statement) and keeps the rest; the three gates read that. Exactly `#[cfg(test)]` is test-only; `cfg(any(test, feature))` items ship and stay. First run over whole files: G1 found `dsm::sofi::signature::verify_signed_object` — the one verifier binding a signed SoFi object's key to an expected signer — has no production caller (the audit's "No production SoFi path binds a precommit's key to the trader it names", MR-SOFI-0151). Baselined by name with that reason; the key-binding change wires it and deletes the line. |
| `ci/sofi_validated_root_constructors.sh` | Counted constructors as literal `-> Self`, so a `Result<Self, …>`/`Option<Self>` constructor was invisible; named `rehydrate_from_admitted_store` as a deliberate puncture and never checked its callers. | Counts all three shapes; enumerates every production caller of `rehydrate_from_admitted_store` (six files, each ruled: this device resuming its own coordinate) and fails on a new one. `sofi_evidence.rs` uses it to label the local device's claim as a foreign trader's (audit, `AcceptedClaim constructable with a caller-chosen identity`); it is listed as a caller and that use is an open finding, not a ruling. |
| `ci/conformance_evidence.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | Nothing ran the gate with `--board-log`, so "Met" was checked as "a test of that name exists"; a binary that crashed before its `test result:` line left its tests counted as passed; a formal property counted as the test a Met row needs. | The main-branch board (`rust-board`) tees its logs and runs the gate on them; a binary without `test result:` leaves every test it started `crashed`; a Met row and a matrix Negative-test cell need a cargo test. Pull requests stay static. |
| `dsm/.cargo/config.toml`, `Cargo.toml`, `deterministic_state_machine/Cargo.toml`, `crates/dsm-android-anchor/Cargo.toml` | `overflow-checks=on` sat in `dsm/.cargo/config.toml`; Cargo reads config from the invocation directory upward, and nothing built from there — CI from the root, Android from `deterministic_state_machine/` and the anchor crate — so integer overflow wrapped silently in every release build and on the board. | `[profile.release] overflow-checks = true` in the three manifests Cargo reads; the unread config file is deleted. |
| `.github/workflows/release.yml` | Staged `target/release/dsm_storage_node`; the binary is `storage_node`. The release job could not have produced an artifact. | Stages `storage_node`. |
| `.githooks/pre-push` | Ran `cargo test --workspace --features local-dev`; the feature does not exist, so the hook always failed and every push needed `--no-verify`. | Runs `make lint` and `ci/production_safety_checks.sh` — the gates that read the tree; the board runs in CI on the pushed commit. |

**Reported, not changed here.** No status check is required to merge (a repository ruleset; the owner's call). The `rust-gates` build and clippy use `--all-features`, which compiles `bitcoin-testnet-bypass` (Bitcoin is parked). `cargo deny` runs only from the Makefile. The Lean job rejects `sorry` and has no axiom inventory. `ci/storage_is_dumb.sh` is a word match; the node routes it does not see (`devtree`, the DLV slot, the genesis forwarder) are a node change, §5A row 5. `ci/sofi_reachability.py` checks column-0 `pub fn` only, and the two completion verifiers in its baseline are the S10 question.

### 6.23 A signed object is recognized only when its signature verifies (shortcut audit, 2026-09-25)

The audit's "Exercise and fulfillment-key recognisers never check signatures" (High, confirmed at `dsm/src/sofi/exercise.rs:52`, `publication.rs:226`, `registration.rs:54`).

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm/src/sofi/publication.rs` · `signed_body`, `recognize_setup`/`recognize_precommit`/`recognize_fulfillment` | Recognition decoded the envelope and copied its signature out unchecked ("Recognition is not verification"). Every reader of a cell or an index — the attempt cells (`exercise_at`), `K_ful(q)` (`names_fulfillment_key`), the locator scans (`keep_verifying`) — took an envelope with any bytes in its signature field as the object. A keyless writer could put a shape-valid exercise first at a successor key's leader and void the honest route that reserved it (rung 5), or stall the vault's ladder with one leader-held copy; a junk-signed `F` first at `K_ful(q)` left the trader's position fenced for good; a junk-signed copy of a trader's setup under its `ρ` made the trader's route Invalid. The test fixtures signed with an 8-byte constant, which passed only because nothing checked. | An envelope is recognized only when its signature verifies over its body's signing digest under the key the body commits (SoFi §9: every object carries its own authority; storage spec §9 rule 3: decidable from the bytes). A signature that does not verify makes the bytes not an object of that kind anywhere. The fixtures sign with the trader's real key. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/exercise.rs` · `recognize_exercise`; `registration.rs` · `names_fulfillment_key` | `F`'s key was never compared with `P`'s at recognition. | An exercise, and a candidate at `K_ful(q)`, count only when `F`'s `(signature_alg, claimant_public_key)` is `P`'s (conformance item 2, decidable in hand). Binding `P`'s key to the trader's identity is not decidable from the bytes and stays MR-SOFI-0151. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/validation.rs` · `setup_valid` | A junk-signed copy of the trader's own setup was Invalid. | Not a setup: not established (`NonVerifyingObject`), never Invalid — one body has exactly one valid envelope, and a copy anyone can publish cannot condemn a route. A setup that verifies under a key other than `P`'s stays Invalid (`NotTheExpectedSigner`). |
| `dsm/src/sofi/signature.rs` module doc | Said the storage member verifies `m_F` at `K_ful` ingress. | The member verifies nothing (storage §9 rule 6); every reader verifies it, through recognition. |

Tests: `dsm::sofi::exercise::tests::an_exercise_counts_only_when_f_and_p_are_signed_under_the_key_p_commits`, `dsm::sofi::registration::tests::only_a_fulfillment_signed_under_its_precommits_key_names_the_key`, `dsm::sofi::publication::tests::an_envelope_is_recognized_only_when_its_signature_verifies_under_its_bodys_key`, `dsm::sofi::validation::tests::a_setup_is_the_traders_only_under_the_key_p_commits`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::an_unsigned_exercise_at_a_successor_key_takes_nothing`. Mutations: signature check removed from `signed_body` → the four Core tests and the e2e red; `F` key = `P` key removed from `recognize_exercise` → the exercise test red; from `names_fulfillment_key` → the registration test red.

### 6.24 A reserve release is constructible only for the amount its release rule names (shortcut audit, 2026-09-25)

The audit's "Faucet reserve can be drained by one throwaway-key release" (High, confirmed at `dsm/src/economic/native_reserve.rs:451`).

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm/src/economic/native_reserve.rs` · `release_constructible` | The reserve's construction predicate — the recognizer at every successor cell — checked reserve, parent, generation, set, zero and overdraft, and nothing else. A release of the whole remaining supply (80,000,000,000 at `R_0`), signed by any key the release itself carries, was a recognized object; first at the leader with two more links it was final, `remaining` became 0, and every later claim failed forever. The payout and signer checks ran only later, in the credit arm, after the cell was decided. Honest claimants even completed the attacker's chain for it (storage §9 rule 8). | A `FaucetClaimant` release is constructible only for `ERA_FAUCET_PAYOUT` (`ReleaseRefusal::NotTheReleaseRulesAmount`): SoFi §51 rule 3, units come out only under the conditions the policy commits, and the beta claim policy fixes the amount. An off-rule release is not an object naming the cell, so it never holds one. The conservation tests release payouts only. |

**Resolved by owner ruling (2026-09-25), §6.26.** The release carries the recipient device's `AttA`, and the cell binds its signer to the device it names from the bytes alone; the recognizer never fetches.

Tests: `dsm::economic::native_reserve::tests::a_release_of_any_amount_but_the_payout_never_holds_the_cell`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::faucet_flow_tests::a_release_of_the_whole_supply_holds_nothing_and_the_claim_lands`, `dsm::native_reserve_wire::credits_funded_along_a_lineage_sum_to_what_the_reserve_released` (a credit funded by an off-rule release is refused). Mutation: the amount check removed → the Core test and the e2e red.

### 6.25 Five more unestablished facts that were read as verdicts (shortcut audit, 2026-09-25)

Storage spec §4 (MR-STOR-0021): a fact that is not established is never read as its negation. DSM Amendment A1: evidence that cannot be obtained yet is not a verdict. Each row was confirmed by the audit's verification pass and is fixed with a test that performs the failure.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm/src/economic/lineage.rs` · `advance_validated`; `provenance.rs`; `peer_lineage.rs` · `step_failure` | The resolver's failure to establish the network's register set carries its class (`Incomplete`, `Invalid`, …); `advance_validated` discarded it for a classless `RegisterNotResolvable`, which the peer walk maps to `Invalid` — permanent and quarantining. An unreadable local catalog branded an honest sender a forger, refused a vault genesis, and cleared the verifier's own peer memo. | `ProvenanceError::RegisterNotEstablished(PeerLineageFailure)` keeps the resolver's class through `advance_validated` and the peer walk (`at_position`). `RegisterNotResolvable` stays for what is decided in hand (unknown network, a candidate that does not re-derive the pinned id). Test: `dsm::economic_admission_lifecycle::a_register_set_not_established_is_not_a_verdict_about_the_claimant`, plus the class assertion in `a_step_failure_keeps_the_class_it_was_established_in`. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/storage_node_sdk.rs` · `answer` | `204 | 404 => Ok(None)`: a `404` (wrong path, a member without the route) counted as the member taking a put, an index append or a mirror sync, and as a healthy member in `storage.status`. No member route answers `404` on success. | `404` is an error. The `.ok()??` readers already read it as "did not answer". Test on the node's own app: the same member at a path it does not serve refuses all four; at its real path it takes them. `dsm_sdk::sdk::storage_node_sdk::tests::a_member_that_answers_404_took_nothing`. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/handlers/online_finalize.rs` · `verify_acceptance_receipt`; `storage_routes.rs` · `verify_inbound_receipt_sig_a` | `.ok().flatten().unwrap_or_else(root AK)`: a cert-chain head that could not be read was taken as "no head yet", and the chain was checked against the root AK — the fallback the send path forbids. An honest delta was parked as rejected; the recipient's `sig_a` check misreported. The unit test for the projection regression passed only through this path (it ran with no store). | Three-way: a head, genesis (`Ok(None)` → the AK), or an error that is not a verdict. The projection test runs on a readable store. Test: `dsm_sdk::handlers::online_finalize::tests::an_unreadable_counterparty_head_is_never_read_as_genesis` (both verifiers, with the head table unreadable and restored). |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/inbox_poller.rs` · `has_pending_settlement_work`, `stop_poller_for_lifecycle`; `jni` · `onAppBackgrounded`; `inbox.stopPoller` | All five settlement queries were `.unwrap_or(false)`, so an unreadable store meant "nothing owed" and the lifecycle stop killed the poller mid-settlement — including `pending_outbound_replies`, which itself fails closed on a malformed row. The JNI hook also read a panic as "let the host go". | `has_pending_settlement_work` returns `Result`; the lifecycle stop declines on an error; the poller keeps the settlement cadence and logs the cause every cycle; the JNI directive releases the host only on a readable "nothing owed"; `inbox.stopPoller` reports the error. Test: `dsm_sdk::sdk::inbox_poller::tests::a_lifecycle_stop_is_declined_while_settlement_state_is_unreadable`. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/b0x_sdk.rs` · `retrieve_from_b0x_v2`, `SpoolCoverage`, `RetrievalOutcome`; `storage_routes.rs` · `storage_sync`, `pull_and_process_inbox`; `inbox_routes.rs` · `inbox.pull` | A retrieval in which no member answered returned `Ok(vec![])`, and `storage.sync` reported `success: true, pulled: 0` and counted the run as completed. A read that reached too few members was the same collapse one level up: one member answering empty is not a globally empty inbox, because a delivery lands on the register quorum of members, not on all of them. | A retrieval no member answered is an error. Otherwise it returns a `RetrievalOutcome` carrying its coverage (owner ruling 2026-09-25): `Complete` once `members − quorum + 1` members answered — by quorum intersection every delivery is on at least one of them — else `Partial { responded, needed }`. A sync processes what it read and pushes what it owes, and when any route was read partially or not at all it fails, names the gap, and is not counted as a completed run. `inbox.pull` never answers "empty" from a partial read. Six outage tests asserted `success` on a sync run while the quorum was broken — the collapse itself — and now assert that the sync was incomplete (`test_support::two_device::assert_incomplete`) beside the state they check. Tests: `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::an_inbox_read_that_did_not_cover_every_delivery_is_not_a_complete_sync` (quorum broken → partial, every member down → no answer, every member back → complete, the completed-run count moving only then); `dsm_sdk::sdk::b0x_sdk::tests::a_read_covers_every_delivery_only_from_the_quorum_intersection_up` (the boundary, 5/3 and 3/2). |

### 6.26 A faucet release occupies a cell only when its signer is the device it credits (owner ruling, 2026-09-25)

§6.24 left the release's signer bound to nothing at the cell: any key could release one payout per generation to a recipient that never credits it, a 100-at-a-time drain that also took each generation from the next honest claimant.

**Ruling.** Carry `AttA`; never fetch in the recognizer. Cell recognition is a deterministic function of the parent and the bytes in hand; a recognizer that fetched identity material would make occupancy depend on availability (the same bytes "not a release" during an outage and "a release" later). The rule is `VerifySig(claimant_public_key, body, σ) ∧ derive_devid(claimant_public_key, AttA) = recipient_devid`. It proves the key is the device's (`DevID = H(AK ‖ AttA)` commits the AK, so same-DevID AK rotation is not a supported operation); it does not prove the device belongs to `recipient_genesis`, which P0–P6 proves when a credit consumes the release. If the invariant must be that anything able to occupy a reserve cell is creditable to the named Genesis, the release needs a self-contained authenticated identity witness — still with no fetch.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `proto/dsm_app.proto` · `FaucetClaimantRecipientV1`; `dsm/src/economic/native_reserve.rs` · `ReleaseSource::FaucetClaimant`, `release_constructible` | The faucet source carried the signer's key and nothing binding it to the recipient. | Field 2, `claimant_att_a` (32 bytes, a wire change). `release_constructible` refuses a release whose `derive_devid(claimant_public_key, claimant_att_a)` is not `recipient_devid` (`ReleaseRefusal::SignerIsNotTheRecipient`). The TypeScript binding is regenerated. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/faucet_claim_flow.rs`; `signing_authority.rs` · `current_att_a` | — | The claimant derives its `AttA` from the wallet seed, `G` and its device slot, and refuses to sign a release if its key and `AttA` do not derive its own device id. |
| test fixtures (`native_reserve.rs`, `native_reserve_wire.rs`, `economic_admission_lifecycle.rs`) | Every fixture named a constant recipient device beside a freshly generated key — a pairing no honest producer can make. | The recipient device is derived from the fixture's key and `AttA`. The whole-supply drain e2e signs under a key bound to the device it names, so only the amount rule refuses it. |

Tests: `dsm::economic::native_reserve::tests::a_release_signed_by_any_key_but_the_recipient_devices_never_holds_the_cell`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::faucet_flow_tests::a_release_naming_a_device_its_key_does_not_derive_holds_nothing`. The existing faucet flow (nine tests, each a real claim through the cell) passes with the binding enforced. Mutation: the binding removed → the Core test and the e2e red.

### 6.27 A precommit's key binds to the parent claim it extends (shortcut audit, 2026-09-25)

The audit's "P's key is never bound to the trader" (High, confirmed at `dsm/src/sofi/conformance.rs:488`, `signature.rs:215`). SoFi §17.1 P conformance rules 2, 3 and 8; §20.2 item 1.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm/src/sofi/conformance.rs` · `fulfillment_conformance`, `parent_binds_the_key` | P verified under the key P itself commits, and item 2 bound F's key to P's: a closed loop. Nothing tied either key to the trader the operation names, so a P naming another trader's `(G, DevID, p)`, with F and every setup signed under any key, conformed. When the parent claim's bytes were in hand as a closure object they were only hash-checked; the claim's key was never read. | Item 1 decides P conformance rules 2, 3 and 8 from the evidence. Single-root parent: the claim at the `claim_ref` P names must verify (`ParentClaimDoesNotVerify`), name P's genesis, device and position and commit `P.void_root` (`ParentClaimIsAnotherPosition`), and be signed under P's algorithm and key (`KeyIsNotTheParentClaimants`). Conditional parent: `C_p` carries no key, so P's key must be the key of the `F` whose `FulfillmentId` P names. That id hashes F's body, key included, so an F that does not re-derive it binds nothing (`ConformanceMissing::ParentFulfillment`). The acquisition fetches it by that id. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/validation.rs` · `validate`; `conformance.rs` | Rule 2's second half, "matches the typed parent reference in `𝒞_E^pre`", was checked nowhere. The producer built every closure empty, so `E` committed to no parent at all. | `ParentClaimRef::validation_ref` is the typed reference. `validate` (the producer's pre-publication check and every route validation) and conformance refuse a P whose parent `𝒞_E^pre` does not reference (`ParentReferenceNotInClosure`). |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/sofi_sdk.rs` · `pre_e_closure`; `sofi_flow.rs` · `exercise_draft` | — | Every draft's closure references the parent P names. The exercise carries the trader's own parent object (`own_closure_objects`), as the completion path already did. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/sofi_advance.rs` · `own_closure_objects` | Read a conditional parent's `C_p` at `K_root(p)` routed by `P.void_root`, the root at `p`. A position's cells are routed by the root it was built on (`cells_of` routes `F.q` by `P.void_root`), so `C_p` was never found. The path was unreached while closures were empty. | Routed by `own_root_before(p)`: the root this device's admitted position `p − 1` selected. |
| test fixtures (`validation::fixtures`, the conformance rig, `exercise.rs`) | Every fixture named a fabricated parent (`token(0x66)`, a 40-byte string as the "claim envelope"). | The swap and close fixtures sign a real root claim at `P_POS` over the trader's pre-root, and their closures reference it. |

**Open (dependency boundary, not closed here).** A verifier binds P's key to the claim P names and checks that claim against P's own `(G, DevID, p, root)`. It does not establish that the claim is the one registered at `K_root(p)`. A foreign verifier cannot route that cell: it needs the root at `p − 1`, which P does not carry. The trader's own verifier uses its frozen claim. The key → device binding that would close this from the bytes in hand (the `AttA` shape of §6.26) is the audit's K_root recognizer finding, and needs an owner ruling on the claim and precommit formats. MR-SOFI-0144 stays Partial on it.

Tests: `dsm::sofi::conformance::tests::a_precommit_under_a_key_its_parent_claim_does_not_carry_does_not_conform`, `dsm::sofi::conformance::tests::a_parent_claim_of_another_position_or_root_does_not_conform`, `dsm::sofi::conformance::tests::a_parent_that_is_not_a_verifying_claim_does_not_conform`, `dsm::sofi::conformance::tests::a_parent_the_closure_does_not_reference_does_not_conform`, `dsm::sofi::conformance::tests::a_conditional_parent_binds_p_to_the_key_of_the_f_that_installed_it`, `dsm::sofi::validation::tests::a_precommit_naming_a_parent_its_closure_does_not_carry_is_invalid`, and the node e2e trades (`dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_sofi_trade_executes_end_to_end`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_key_held_by_an_exercise_its_own_bytes_refute_is_skipped_on_those_bytes_alone` — a second trade on a resolved conditional parent). Mutations, each observed red on its named test: rule 2 removed from `validate` and from conformance; the single-root key check; each of the genesis, device, position and root checks; the conditional key, position and id checks; the claim's signature check; the producer's closure emptied; the parent F not fetched; `C_p` routed by `P.void_root`.

### 6.28 The placeholder and wiring sweep (#992–#1008, 2026-09-25)

Owner request: find every placeholder or fake piece, and check that everything is connected where it needs to be. Three read-only sweeps (Core; SDK; node, wire and JNI) ran against `8e51f121`, and each High finding was re-read at its source. Owner rulings of the same day: BLE is a transport only; online and offline bilateral are two separate live processes, and the offline protocol is transport-agnostic, decided in Core and run by the SDK; Core derives the SoFi verdict; the preloaded ERA policy is deleted last and leaves its hole.

**Resolved (#992)**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/inbox_poller.rs · `decode_sync_response` | `storage.sync` answers its caller with a local answer (no headers); the poller decoded it as an addressed envelope, so every cycle read (0, 0) and the poller never announced an arrival. Its tests used a headered envelope production never sends. | Decoded as the local answer it is; the tests build it with `pack_envelope_ok`. |
| `dsm_storage_node` · api/transport/b0x.rs; db/pg.rs | `message_id` was UNIQUE across spools, so an envelope reusing an id was dropped and answered 204; the node decoded envelopes on the way in and out, and one undecodable row stopped a spool read. | The spool stores and returns bytes unopened (storage §8); no uniqueness on the id. Schema version 2. |
| `dsm_storage_node` · api/objects/bytecommit.rs · `mirror_sync` | A failed set-mate reported "0 new". | 204 when every set-mate answered, 409 in no set, 502 when a set-mate did not answer or is not the member configured. |
| `dsm_storage_node` · gossip, discovery, admin maintenance, device-tree mirror, genesis forwarder, DLV slot, timing, hardening | Gossip sent no token and every peer answered 401 while maintenance answered `ok: true`; every node derived the same id from its bind address; the other surfaces had no client, judged content or ignored a re-PUT. | Deleted with their proto messages and config keys. The database requires TLS unless its URL says `sslmode=disable` (a "localhost" substring used to select `prefer`, which falls back to plaintext). |
| `dsm` · core/identity; `dsm_sdk`; wire; Android; frontend | The storage-node MPC genesis ceremony was still in the tree beside the mnemonic-rooted Genesis v3. | Removed everywhere; `genesis_records` holds what the mnemonic path produces (client schema 20). |

**Resolved (#993)**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk` · storage/client_db/contacts.rs · `store_contact`, the row reader, `get_contact_chain_tip`, `get_local_bilateral_chain_tip` | A contact could be stored without its AK, its Kyber key or its tip (a logged warning); four row readers turned undecodable metadata into an empty map, NULL keys into empty keys, an unreadable status into "Created"; a short tip, a database error or a malformed device id read as "no tip"; NULL/zero tips were "first use". | The store refuses a contact without its directory keys and its 32-byte `h_0`; one strict row reader; the tip readers answer `Result<Option<_>>`, `None` only for no contact. The first-use branches are gone: the relationship is established when the contact is added (ruling of 2026-09-24). Core's `ChainTipStore` read is fallible. |
| `dsm_sdk` · bluetooth/bilateral_ble_handler.rs · prepare and prepare-response | A peer's wire AK and Kyber key were bound "first write wins" and a substitution was only logged. | `require_pinned_peer_keys`: the wire keys must equal the contact's pinned keys and the Kyber binding must verify under the pinned AK; nothing the message carries is written. |

The tip write was a blind overwrite whose comment called it a compare-and-set. An empty token id settled as ERA; a sender's repeated settlement answered a made-up default outcome; the projection read the head from the process-global router; `available` was a saturating `balance − locked`. The tip is a compare-and-set bound to the step's commitment (`contacts.chain_tip_commitment`, client schema 23): it moves only from the step's parent, a replay is recognised as already applied and refused, anything else is a conflict. An empty token is refused; a lock above the balance is an error; the sender idempotency table and the dead bundles are gone. |

The receiver signs its receipt from the advance that commits — prepared, signed and committed under the state-machine lock, with no other advance in between (`execute_offline_step`'s `before_commit`) — and its commit writes the credit, the tip, the history with that receipt, both EK chain heads (each a compare-and-set from the head the step was verified against or signed from), the anchor frontier, both EK step objects (bound to the root the commit produced) and the session's end. The sender's commit writes the same with its stashed EK. The unconditional head writers are deleted; their tests drive the compare-and-set writers. |

Each side keeps the rejection or cancellation that ended the step as the answer to the other's next frame for it, so no frame is pushed forever. The Lean module proves both-or-neither for one step over every behaviour from four inductive clauses, and for each flipped rule exhibits a behaviour that breaks it. `DSM_BilateralLiveness` loses the failure on disconnect or timeout and the recovery; its termination excepts a device of the pair that dies. |

**Resolved (#1008)**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk` · the Android-target unit tests (jni/helpers.rs, jni/unified_protobuf_bridge.rs); CI · Android Instrumented Tests | They did not compile (21 errors recorded; the last three: a Base32 test matching a `Result` where decode returns `Option`, and a test calling a function its module did not import), and nothing built them. | They compile, and the Android job compiles them with the library's profile and features. |
**Open**

- A step's commitment binds its parent tip and its operation, not its proposer: a direction-less operation (Noop, Generic) proposed by each device on one tip is one commitment, each device answers the other's prepare as its own proposal (with nothing), and both stay Prepared until cancelled. A transfer names its recipient and never collides. Liveness only.
- Terminal session rows are pruned to 25 per counterparty (`prune_terminal_sessions_for_counterparty`). A kept rejection or cancellation pruned before a late copy of its step's frame arrives is decided afresh: a refused proposal could then be held, accepted and never answered. Liveness only; it needs 25 terminal steps with the contact between a frame and its late copy.
- The doors' check and write are one critical section in one process (`STEP_DOOR`); no test forces the interleaving it excludes.
- The frontend `AdditionalDeviceScreen` calls the deleted `device.*` routes; it is the frontend sweep's, and the proto messages it builds stay until then. Resolved on `fix/frontend-storage-integration` (§6.29).
- `message.send` takes its sender and tip from the caller and nothing receives it: build the receive side or delete the feature (owner).
- A proposer's cancellation has no frontend control yet: `cancelBilateralByCommitment` reaches the WebView bridge, Kotlin and the handler, and the frontend sweep adds the control. `TxTypeBilateralOfflineRecovered` is no longer produced; its enum value and the frontend badge retire with the frontend sweep. Both resolved on `fix/frontend-sweep-continued` (§6.29).
- `wallet.sendOffline` and `bilateral.prepare` build an operation from UI hints; a BLE event whose token's decimals are unknown carries no display amount; `sync_balance_cache` logs a failed reload; the BLE handler reads the process-global router rather than its own. All four belong to the offline engine (C5a of the sweep plan).
- Nothing runs `dsm_sdk`'s Android-target unit tests on a device; CI compiles them (#1008).
- `dsm_sdk` · bluetooth/anchor_accept.rs · `DsmStateVerifier::verify_transition` answers `true`: Def. 12 step 12 (σ^DSM over the transition and the consumption R_i → R_{i+1}) is not checked inside the offline-release predicate. The relationship path rides in the stitched receipt, which the handler verifies before the predicate runs, so the check rests on caller order. Closing it means carrying the relationship proofs in the release (a wire change in the anchor subsystem); the design is the owner's. `SphincsPart::part_keygen`/`part_sign` answer empty values for trait methods the receiver never calls.
- A device that advances between building its confirm and committing — another relationship's step, a contact added — cannot commit the step: its root is no longer the root the receiver verified, so the step fails and the relationship is held. Nothing holds the head for an open step.
- JNI exports for Bitcoin with no Kotlin declaration (`bitcoinGenerateAddress`, `bitcoinSwapInitiate`, `bitcoinSwapComplete`, `bitcoinSwapRefund`, `bitcoinSwapStatus`, `bitcoinVerifyPayment`): Bitcoin is parked, so they are recorded, not changed.

Tests: `dsm_sdk::sdk::inbox_poller::tests::a_storage_sync_answer_as_the_router_frames_it_is_read`, `dsm_storage_node::api::transport::b0x::tests::an_envelope_reusing_a_message_id_is_kept_after_the_first`, `dsm_storage_node::api::transport::b0x::tests::bytes_the_node_cannot_read_are_kept_and_returned_unopened`, `dsm_storage_node::bytecommit_chain::a_node_in_no_set_refuses_a_mirror_sync`, `dsm_storage_node::bytecommit_chain::an_unreachable_set_mate_fails_the_sync`, `dsm_sdk::storage::client_db::contacts::tests::a_contact_without_its_keys_or_its_tip_is_refused`, `dsm_sdk::storage::client_db::contacts::tests::a_corrupt_stored_tip_is_an_error_not_an_absent_tip`, `dsm_sdk::storage::client_db::contacts::tests::a_device_id_that_is_not_32_bytes_is_an_error_not_an_absent_contact`, `dsm::bilateral::offline::tests::a_peer_is_held_to_the_keys_its_contact_pins`, `dsm_sdk::storage::client_db::token_registry::tests::tampered_policy_bytes_are_an_error`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::wallet_routes::tests::a_token_without_a_registry_entry_has_no_decimals`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::online_finalize::tests::a_receipt_naming_other_roots_than_the_committed_ones_is_rejected`, `dsm::envelope::tests::a_payload_the_proto_defines_passes_the_strict_validator`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::bilateral_ble_handler::tests::a_sender_commit_that_fails_its_checks_settles_nothing_and_holds_the_relationship`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::bilateral_ble_handler::tests::a_commit_ack_for_a_session_this_device_is_not_committing_finalizes_nothing`. Mutations, each observed red on its named test: the addressed decoder restored; decode restored on submit; the id dedupe restored; a partial and a set-less sync answered 204; the Kyber key not required; a short and a NULL tip read as absent; the wire Kyber key not held to the pin; the policy hash check disabled; an unknown token read as 0 decimals; payloads from 107 refused again by the validator; a refused sender commit marked Committed, or not held for reconcile; an ack for a failed or unknown session let through. the confirm's row not written in its transaction; the in-flight check removed. #1000: `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_lost_ack_is_answered_again_and_the_step_commits_once_on_each_side`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_committed_steps_confirm_not_signed_by_its_sender_is_not_answered`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_response_delivered_again_is_answered_with_the_confirm_it_owes`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_prepare_delivered_again_to_an_acceptance_is_answered_with_its_response`, `dsm::bilateral::offline::tests::a_committed_steps_confirm_is_answered_only_as_its_sender_signed_it`; Formal: TLC on `DSM_OfflineFinality.cfg` 4,481 distinct states clean with liveness; each falsification config red on its invariant and both witnesses reached; `SessionTermination` red locally with the lost-ack re-answer removed, and with the refusal not kept; two steps each way, safety only, 18,192,082 distinct states clean (local, too large for the gate); `DSM_BilateralLiveness` 1,256,040 distinct states clean with its three liveness properties (opt-in set); Lean kernel-checked with `-DwarningAsError=true`, red with each rule mutated into the code's rules and with each invariant clause dropped.

### 6.29 The frontend sweep, storage first (`fix/frontend-storage-integration` #1002, then `fix/frontend-sweep-continued`, 2026-09-25)

Owner request: integrate the frontend with the storage nodes properly, working from the UI toward the backend. Owner rule restated: all logic is in Rust (SDK, then Core); Kotlin carries bytes as the transport and hardware bridge; what comes from hardware reaches Rust before the frontend, and the frontend reaches hardware only through Rust. The frontend renders.

Kotlin's `runNativeBridgeSelfTest` pushed empty envelopes through `processEnvelopeV3`/`processBleChunk` and was reachable from nothing. | Every line of the report is a measurement or names the failure of measuring it: the native session phase, the identity from Rust's transport headers (`getIdentity`), the architecture check as the native checker answered it or `arch=not measured: <error>`, and the errors this session saw; the bundle's log section says `not read: <error>` when the log was not read. `getArchitectureInfo` refuses a status the checker cannot measure; Kotlin's handler lets its failure reach the RPC wrapper. The phantom helpers, `maybeThrowOnEmpty`, the window globals, the router gate and stub detection, the phantom declarations, telemetry's fallbacks and swallow, `hasUserConsentFromPrefs` and Kotlin's self-test are deleted; `BridgeGate` gates on the bridge object alone, which is all it ever did. |

- frontend · SofiScreen: orders a vault's token pair bytewise before sending it (§28) — a protocol rule applied above Rust. The SoFi track's screen.

The dBTC entry is not touched: Bitcoin is parked.
- frontend · dsm/transactions.ts `schedulePostAcceptRefreshes`: after Accept, four re-reads of the wallet on a frame cadence (0/0.5/1/2 s) beside Rust's TRANSFER_COMPLETE announcement, kept because whether the announcement alone reaches the screen on a device is undecided; a device run decides, and the cadence goes if it does.
- frontend · services/recovery/nfcRecoveryService.ts `getNfcBackupStatus`: reads `recovery.status` as `key=value` text inside an `AppStateResponse` and fills a missing `capsule_count`/`last_capsule_index` with 0 — a text protocol on a DSM path. Recovery is a dependency boundary this round; the route and its reader change together when the recovery specification is in scope.
- frontend · `public/index.html` `sendMessageBin`: rejects a request after 30 minutes on a wall-clock timer (a bound sized for the M=3 K=21 enrollment); `installPortHandler` tells a response from an async event by whether the first eight bytes match a pending id. Both are transport-side, fail-closed and unchanged by the sweep; they are where the port protocol still guesses.
- Kotlin · androidTest `AndroidLayerProofTest.claimFaucet`: hand-encodes the ArgPack's `schema_hash` as 32 zero bytes, where the frontend sends none, and swallows the claim's failure.

### 6.30 The SoFi verdict is Core's: the reads, the facts and the ladder (`fix/sofi-verdict-core-resolver`, 2026-09-26)

Owner ruling (plan of 2026-09-25, restated 2026-09-26 — "get the real wire in"): the SoFi verdict is Core-derived; Core decides what is read and what it means, the SDK answers reads.

**Finding.** `dsm::sofi::lineage::advance_resolved(.., resolution: Resolution, receipt)` installed `P.realize_root` (and moved balances) or the predecessor's root on whatever `Resolution` the caller passed; nothing bound that value to the ladder. The SDK decided what to read (`sofi_resolve.rs`, `sofi_chain.rs`, `sofi_evidence.rs`, `sofi_register.rs`), ran the ladder itself over a `RouteFacts` it assembled from plain values — `registered` from its own comparison, bare `Validation` values for conformance and validation, each leg's `CellFact`, a `ParentStatus` read off a `VaultChain` whose `pub roots` were filled from local rows never checked against anything, `attempt_live` from its own reading of a `WalkOutcome` — and re-stated the answer to Core. `Void` needed no evidence at all. A forged Void un-spends a consumed debit leg on the trader's own lineage; a forged Realized credits an unconsumed route. Peers catch neither today: the peer walk refuses a resolved SoFi position (P15-9), so the damage was confined to the forger's own head.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm/src/sofi/lineage.rs` · `advance_resolved` | Took the resolution from the caller; a realized advance took its evidence from a caller-built `RealizedReceipt`; `RegisteredClaims.conditional` compared a value the SDK read against the derived `C_q`. | Takes `Established` — the facts Core built, or the exercise's in-hand refutation — and the receiving head; runs `resolve_position` itself; installs on that answer alone. `Resolution` is an argument of nothing that installs. A realized advance derives credits and balances from the evidence inside the facts, the same bytes the verdict was reached on. `RealizedReceipt` and `RegisteredClaims` deleted. Tests: `dsm::sofi::lineage::tests::the_installed_root_follows_the_ladder_over_the_facts`, `facts_the_ladder_does_not_resolve_install_nothing`, `a_refuted_exercise_installs_nothing`, `facts_established_for_another_operation_are_refused`, `a_realized_advance_without_the_evidence_it_was_reached_on_is_refused`. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/resolve.rs` (new) · `Verifier` over `SofiReads` | The SDK decided what was read and in what order: the position pair, the attempt cells, the objects conformance and validation consume, a vault's genesis and the walk of its chain, with acquisition rounds and budgets of its own. | The verifier is Core's, in the shape of the peer lineage walker: `Verifier::{read_registration, read_attempt_cell, acquire_conformance_evidence, acquire_evidence, vault_genesis, chain, walk_parent, continue_walk, establish_own}` decide every read over a `SofiReads` the SDK implements with bytes, cells and this device's own rows (`dsm_sdk/src/sdk/sofi_reads.rs` · `LiveSofiReads`, `block_in_place` on the multi-thread runtime, as `LiveRegisterResolver` does). Every cell is derived by Core from committed state, every read evaluated by Core, every object recognized by Core, every predicate recomputed by Core, every budget Core's (`WALK_BUDGET`, `CHAIN_DEPTH`, `SIBLING_DEPTH`, `GENERATION_BUDGET`, `ACQUIRE_ROUNDS`, `PRIOR_ATTEMPT_BUDGET`, `NAMED_PRECOMMIT_BUDGET`). `LocalLeaves`, `VaultGenesis`, `Acquired` moved into Core. `sofi_resolve.rs`, `sofi_chain.rs`, `sofi_evidence.rs` deleted; `sofi_register.rs` keeps the install only; `sofi_exercise.rs` keeps the build and the write. On the nodes: `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::*` (the resolve path end to end), `dsm_sdk::sdk::sofi_reads::tests::an_unestablished_genesis_candidate_is_not_read_as_unpublished`. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/facts.rs` (new) · `establish`, `refuted_in_hand`, `Established::refuted` | The facts were SDK-assembled. | Core builds them from reads Core evaluated, each bound to what it was read at: a `RegistrationRead` (trader, position, `R_p`); an `AttemptCellRead` per leg (`(v, R, a)`); an `AttemptWalk` bound to `(v, R)` from key zero, for liveness; the vault's `VaultChain`; conformance and validation recomputed over the evidence; the parent from this device's own admitted position. A read of another position or key is `NotEstablished::NotThisExercise`, never a fact. Tests on real cells and the fixture's signed exercise: `dsm::sofi::facts::tests::a_consumed_route_is_established_from_its_reads_and_installs_the_realize_root`, `reads_of_another_position_or_key_establish_nothing`, `an_unregistered_position_is_not_resolved`, `a_parent_the_chain_refutes_voids_and_one_it_has_not_placed_waits`, `an_open_leg_keeps_the_position_unresolved`, `liveness_above_attempt_zero_is_the_walk_that_reached_it`, `a_refuted_position_is_bound_to_its_exercise_and_its_registration`. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/resolution.rs` · `RouteFacts`, `LegFacts`, `KeyFacts`, `VaultChain`, `walk` | Every field public; `walk` classified whatever facts it was handed; `VaultChain.roots` was a public `Vec`. | Fields `pub(crate)` (the ladder's own tests state facts; nothing outside the crate can). `KeyFacts::of` / `KeyFacts::refuted` bind a cell read and the facts of the exercise it holds to the key; `walk` takes the chain's coordinates, treats facts bound to another key as no fact (`a_walk_takes_only_facts_bound_to_the_key_it_asks_about`), and returns an `AttemptWalk` whose `liveness_of` is the one reading of a leg's liveness (`a_walk_establishes_liveness_only_as_far_as_it_reached`). `VaultChain::from_genesis`, `extend` (a post state built on the head only), `from_recorded` (below). `resolve_position` and `resolve_refuted_in_hand` are `pub(crate)`. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/resolution.rs` · `VaultChain::from_recorded`; `dsm_sdk/src/storage/client_db/sofi_vault_head.rs` (schema 24) | The recorded roots of a vault were read back as the chain, unchecked: a `Vec` of roots from `sofi_vault_root` rows, which `record_resolved_with_conn` overwrote on conflict. | The rows record, past genesis, the root each generation was built on and the `E` of the operation that consumed it (`VaultPostState::consumed_by`). The chain walk reads the genesis from the network every time and stands on the memo only anchored at that accepted genesis and linked row to row (`from_recorded`: row zero is the accepted root, every later row is built on the one before it and names its consumption, generations are contiguous; anything else is `MemoBroken`, a refusal, never a chain). The writer refuses a different root or a different link at an established generation (`write_root`), so a record is written once and stands. Tests: `dsm::sofi::resolution::tests::the_memo_becomes_a_chain_only_anchored_at_the_genesis_and_linked_row_to_row`, `dsm_sdk::storage::client_db::sofi_vault_head::tests::an_established_generation_is_never_rewritten`, `the_recorded_generations_are_read_contiguously_with_their_links`. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/exercise.rs` · `attempt_resolution`; `registration.rs` · `fulfillment_registered`, `PositionCells`; `validation.rs` · `VaultPostState` | Returned plain values (`CellReading`, `Registration`); `VaultPostState` had public fields. | `AttemptCellRead` and `RegistrationRead`, bound to what they were read at (`PositionCells` keeps `R_p`); `VaultPostState` private fields, built by `vault_post_states` only, which is what `VaultChain::extend` grows by. |
| `ci/sofi_validated_root_constructors.sh` §5 | — | Pins: no fact type has a field visible outside the crate; `advance_resolved` takes no `Resolution` and runs the ladder; `VaultChain::from_recorded` has one production caller, the verifier's chain walk; the unchecked memo constructor is gone; `EstablishedFacts` is stated as a literal nowhere in production. |
| `tla/DSM_SofiFulfillment.tla`, `lean4/DSMSofiAtomicity.lean` | The models stated the ladder but took the installed root as a free value (`ValidatedThrough (res : Nat → Resolution)`), the shape of the code defect. | TLA: `installed` and the `Install` action; invariant `InstalledRootIsTheLadders` in the base config (10.2M states, no error); mutation `InstallFromCaller` (`_InstallOnCallerValue.cfg`, expected violation) and non-vacuity `_InstallReachable.cfg`, both registered in `tla_runner.rs`. Lean: `installedRoot` with `realize_root_installed_iff_ladder_realized`, `void_root_installed_iff_ladder_void`, `nothing_installed_iff_ladder_selects_no_root`; `ValidatedThroughFacts` over the ladder with `advance_on_the_ladder_extends_validated_lineage` and `no_install_extends_nothing`. |

| Location | Hole |
|---|---|
| `dsm/src/sofi/resolve.rs` · `Verifier::chain` | What a recorded generation memoizes — that the consumption it names happened — is not re-established from the network on a later walk; the memo is anchored at the accepted genesis and linked, and that is what can be checked without walking again. The same class as the peer walker's memo start. |
| `dsm/src/economic/provenance.rs` · P15-9 | The peer walk still refuses a resolved SoFi position. The verifier that could establish one is Core's now; wiring it into the peer walk (a `Verifier` over the peer's position, with the peer's own leaves as its `NoSource`) is that round's work. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/resolve.rs` · `acquire_evidence` | Another trader's route remains unresolvable by a verifier that is not the trader (`Acquired::NoSource`): `TraderSideValid` reads the trader's leaf pre values and no section names where a foreign verifier gets them (§6.5). |

### 6.31 The SoFi facade reports what happened (placeholder sweep C1, `fix/sofi-facade-outcomes-classified`, 2026-09-26)

Owner clarification of 2026-09-23 (§6.21): a predicate has two values and the network status is separate. `RetriesExhausted` is that status — the predicates held and the network did not take or decide the position within the rounds — and nothing else is it. Three places in `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/sofi_flow.rs` reported something else as it, or reported an error as an absence, or checked a precondition after the write it guarded.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/sofi_flow.rs` · `exercise_draft`; `sofi_advance.rs` · `complete_pending_fulfillment`, `install_pair`, `exercise_legs`, `fulfill` | Every error of `complete_pending_fulfillment` after the transition — the fence refusing, a non-conforming `F`, an object the network established absent, this device's own store failing — was answered `RetriesExhausted`: a refusal reported as the network status. Underneath, `install_pair` turned every `InstallError`, the leader's silence included, into an `InvalidOperation` refusal, so the same silence was an error from `fulfill` (which installed after its advance) and the network status one stage later; a leg write that did not reach its leader was `Completed`. | `fulfill` is stage 6, the transition, alone; stages 7 and 8 are `complete_pending_fulfillment` for the device that just advanced and the one that comes back from a crash alike. It answers `Completion::Written` or `Completion::NotTaken { position, why: NotTaken }` — the pending objects not established this pass (`Unavailable`), conformance evidence not in hand after the rounds (`Evidence`), the pair's leader silent (`PairLeaderUnreached`), a leg's leader silent (`LegLeaderUnreached`) — and an error for everything else; `install_failure` is the one mapping from `InstallError`. `complete_and_settle` (trade, close, resolve) logs a `NotTaken` and still reads the position as storage holds it: another party may have carried the pair or the exercise (§33); `settle` reports `RetriesExhausted` after its rounds. A refusal propagates as the error it is. Tests: `dsm_sdk::sdk::sofi_advance::tests::only_an_install_the_network_did_not_take_is_the_network_status`; on the nodes, `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_trade_cut_short_by_a_refused_write_is_the_network_status_until_it_lands` (the trade itself is `RetriesExhausted` at `q` with the pair's leader refusing, completion names the pair's leader, then the leg, then is written; a resolve with nothing pending is refused). |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/sofi_flow.rs` · `create_vault`, `setup`; `economic_admission_flow.rs` · `stage_admission`, `BuiltOn` | `create_vault` computed `create_position` from the validated predecessor, published a genesis naming it, ran the admission and only THEN compared the admitted position with the one the genesis names — after the advance, which is irreversible. The two differ whenever the predecessor moves between the two reads, and `stage_admission` moves it on its way in when a pending admission is resumed: the creation was admitted at a position its genesis does not name, then refused. `setup` had the same two reads and no check at all. | `stage_admission` takes the predecessor the operation's object was built on (`BuiltOn`, position and root) and refuses before the advance unless the staged predecessor is exactly it; `create_vault` and `setup` pass theirs, `token.create` and `token.burn` name none. The post-advance comparison is gone; the created vault reports the admitted position. Test on the nodes: `dsm_sdk::handlers::sender_admission_tests::a_creation_built_on_a_predecessor_the_device_no_longer_stands_on_is_refused_before_its_advance` (a held token creation resumed by staging moves the predecessor from 1 to 2; the vault creation built on 1 is refused naming both, admitted stays 2 with nothing pending; built again it is admitted at 3, the position its genesis names). |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/sofi_flow.rs` · `find_route` | A vault whose head could not be established — the fleet not answering, the genesis scan incomplete, the owner's lineage unresolved, this device's record unable to reproduce the head — was logged and left out, and the search answered with whatever remained: an unreachable vault read as "no route", an empty list where the truth was an error. | A vault whose head cannot be established is an error: a search that could not see every vault this device is set up with has not searched. Over established heads, no route is still an empty list. Test on the nodes: `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_route_search_that_cannot_see_a_vault_is_an_error_not_an_empty_route`. |

### 6.32 The ERA policy stand-in deleted, the hole left; the enforcer keyed by the commitment (placeholder sweep, `fix/era-policy-stand-in-deleted`, 2026-09-26)

Owner decision (plan of 2026-09-25): delete the fake ERA policy and leave the hole. The last commit of the sweep, after the clean checkpoint (#1015's board): every test it turns red is named below, and from here a board is judged against that list — a red outside it is a regression, and a listed test that goes green without ERA's policy is a finding.

**Finding.** `CoreSDK::new_with_device` preloaded an "ERA Token Policy" `PolicyFile` of three metadata strings, registered under the ticker `"ERA"` at an anchor that is the hash of that projection — not `ERA_POLICY_COMMIT`, which has no preimage: no `TokenPolicyV3` bytes hash to it, because ERA's release rule is its emission schedule (SoFi §51) and no such blob exists. The enforcer was keyed by the operation's `token_id` string; `register_token_policy_with_anchor` took a `PolicyFile` and an anchor from its caller and bound them on the caller's word; the validator checked semver strings over that projection; and four condition kinds the policy grammar (§47–§54) does not name — an identity allowlist over caller-stated strings, an emission schedule, a credit bundle, a custom constraint — plus role-based access control existed in the type, the enforcer and the proto, three of them "configuration-only" arms that allowed unconditionally.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm/src/core/token/policy/mod.rs` · `TokenPolicySystem` | The ERA stand-in (`preload_standard_policies`, `create_root_token_policy`); registration keyed by ticker, binding a caller-built `PolicyFile` to a caller-supplied anchor; a ticker-keyed `PolicyCommitResolver`. | Deleted. `register_policy(bytes)` recomputes the commitment from the `TokenPolicyV3` bytes, reads them with Core's one parser and derives the enforcer's view (`enforced_policy`, moved in from the SDK's `derive_policy_file`); `policy_at(commit)` takes the durable bytes the SDK resolver answers only when they re-hash to the commitment asked for; `enforce_policy(commit, ..)` denies where no policy is committed. Tests: `dsm::core::token::policy::tests::a_policy_is_registered_from_its_committed_bytes_alone`, `bytes_at_another_commitment_are_not_the_policy_asked_for`, `a_cache_miss_takes_the_durable_bytes_that_re_hash_to_the_commitment`, `an_operation_naming_a_commitment_without_a_policy_is_denied`. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/core_sdk.rs` · `enforce_policy_for_operation`, `build_token_policy_context`; `handlers/token_routes.rs` | Enforcement was keyed by the operation's `token_id` string; creation and rehydration registered a projection under the token id; the resolver mapped a string (canonical id or ticker) to bytes. | Enforcement is keyed by the `policy_commit` the operation carries (Transfer, Burn, CreateToken); a lock operation, which carries none, is refused for it (§9.1). Creation and rehydration hand Core the bytes (`register_policy_bytes`) and take the commitment Core answers; the resolver answers `load_policy_verified(commit)`. `PolicyCommitResolver` deleted. |
| `dsm/src/types/policy_types.rs`, `core/token/policy/policy_enforcement.rs`, `policy_validation.rs`, `policy_cache.rs`; `proto/dsm_app.proto` | `IdentityConstraint` (caller-stated identity strings, "derived identity" by path suffix), `EmissionsSchedule`, `CreditBundlePolicy` and `Custom` conditions, roles and role permissions, the semver validator, the ticker index of the cache; `StoredPolicy`. | Deleted; `PolicyConditionProto` fields 1, 5, 6, 7 and `CanonicalPolicy.roles` reserved (`PolicyRoleProto`, `IdentityConstraintProto`, `EmissionsScheduleProto`, `CreditBundlePolicyProto`, `CustomConstraintProto`, `StoredPolicy` gone; TypeScript bindings regenerated). A committed policy states its recipient allowlist and its release rule in the blob. `BitcoinTapConstraint` stays untouched (Bitcoin is out of this round): its arm allows unconditionally and is recorded below. Test: `dsm::types::policy_types::tests::a_policy_carrying_a_condition_the_grammar_does_not_name_does_not_decode`. |

**Open**

| Location | Hole |
|---|---|
| `dsm/src/core/token/token_state_manager.rs` · `ERA_POLICY_COMMIT` | ERA has no committed policy: no `TokenPolicyV3` bytes hash to its commitment, and the policy grammar has no encoding for its release rule (the emission schedule, §51 — emissions are out of this round). Until the blob exists, every ERA transfer and burn is refused at enforcement ("no policy is committed at the commitment the operation names"). The tests this turns red are listed in the expected-red manifest below. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/validation.rs` · `market_legs_permitted` | The same absence at SoFi validation: ERA and dBTC are exempted as "pre-rooted" and read no policy bytes, so a SoFi leg in ERA is checked against nothing where every other token is checked against its committed policy. Recorded; not changed in this cut. |
| `dsm/src/core/token/policy/policy_enforcement.rs` · `BitcoinTapConstraint` | A configuration-only arm that allows unconditionally, kept because Bitcoin is not touched in this round. |

**Expected-red manifest.** Recorded from the board of this commit; see the pull request body.

| MR-SOFI-0316 | Partial | `dsm::economic::token_policy::ReleaseRule`; `dsm::economic::write_set::build_write_set`; `dsm::economic::native_reserve::release_constructible` | `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_created_token_releases_its_whole_genesis_supply_to_its_creator`; `dsm::economic::native_reserve::tests::a_release_of_any_amount_but_the_payout_never_holds_the_cell`; `dsm_sdk::handlers::faucet_flow_tests::a_release_of_the_whole_supply_holds_nothing_and_the_claim_lands`; `dsm::native_reserve_wire::credits_funded_along_a_lineage_sum_to_what_the_reserve_released`; `dsm::economic::native_reserve::tests::a_release_signed_by_any_key_but_the_recipient_devices_never_holds_the_cell`; `dsm_sdk::handlers::faucet_flow_tests::a_release_naming_a_device_its_key_does_not_derive_holds_nothing` | The only token release rule is `AllAtCreation`, under which no unit stays unreleased; a release formula for any other rule, recomputable by anyone, has no producer. The earlier citation of `handle_token_mint` names code deleted in #977. The native reserve's construction predicate enforces the beta claim policy's amount: a faucet release is constructible only for `ERA_FAUCET_PAYOUT`, so a release the policy does not allow never holds a cell (§6.24). The signer is bound to the recipient device at the cell (`derive_devid(claimant_public_key, AttA) == recipient_devid`, §6.26); that the device belongs to `recipient_genesis` is proven by P0–P6 when a credit consumes the release. |
